the photocentre-vs-aperture curve, the zero-aperture position and a draft ADES record out. Everything runs in this page; nothing is uploaded.</div>\n\n<h2>1. The cutout</h2>\n<div class="row"><input type="file" id="file" accept=".fits,.fit,.fts,.fz,.gz"> <select id="hdu"></select> <span id="info" class="muted"></span></div>\n<div class="row muted" id="hdrnote"></div>\n<div class="row muted">Click the comet to set the start (default: the brightest 5&times;5 pixel).</div>\n<canvas id="img" width="400" height="400"></canvas>\n<div class="row">start x <input type="number" id="sx" step="0.1"> y <input type="number" id="sy" step="0.1"> <span class="muted">(0-based pixels: x = column, y = row)</span></div>\n\n<h2>2. The method</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>estimator <select id="est"><option value="G">G: symmetric Gaussian fit</option><option value="M">M: moment photocentre</option></select></label>\n  <label>radii from <input type="number" id="r0" value="2.0" step="0.1"> to <input type="number" id="r1" value="6.0" step="0.1"> step <input type="number" id="rs" value="0.1" step="0.05"> px</label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>background <select id="bgm"><option value="annulus">annulus 2.5r&ndash;5r</option><option value="value">known value</option></select></label>\n  <input type="number" id="bgv" value="0" step="any">\n</div>\n\n<h2>3. The record (every value is yours; nothing defaults)</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>station <input type="text" id="stn" placeholder="e.g. 568"></label>\n  <label>designation <input type="text" id="desig" placeholder="3I, 1P, C/2025 N1"></label>\n  <label>mode <select id="mode"><option value="">choose</option><option value="CCD">CCD</option><option value="CMO">CMO (CMOS)</option><option value="VID">VID (video)</option><option value="TDI">TDI</option></select></label>\n  <label>catalogue <input type="text" id="astcat" list="cats" placeholder="e.g. Gaia3"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>submitter <input type="text" id="submitter" style="width:11em"></label>\n  <label>measurers <input type="text" id="measurers" style="width:14em" placeholder="e.g. Smith, J.; Doe, A."></label>\n  <label>observers <input type="text" id="observers" style="width:14em" placeholder="optional; e.g. Roe, B."></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>telescope design <input type="text" id="design" placeholder="e.g. Reflector"></label>\n  <label>aperture (m) <input type="text" id="aperture" style="width:4em"></label>\n  <label>detector <input type="text" id="detector" placeholder="e.g. CMOS"></label>\n  <label>observatory name <input type="text" id="obsname" style="width:11em" placeholder="optional"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row"><label>mid-exposure (UTC) <input type="text" id="tobs" style="width:15em" placeholder="from the header, or type it"></label></div>\n<datalist id="cats"></datalist>\n<div class="row"><button id="run">Measure</button> <span id="status" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>4. The answer</h2>\n<div class="grid">\n  <div><svg id="plot" width="460" height="300" viewBox="0 0 460 300"></svg></div>\n  <div><table id="tab"></table></div>\n</div>\n<div class="row" id="zero"></div>\n<pre id="ades"></pre>\n<div class="row muted" id="adesnote"></div>\n<div class="row"><button id="copy" disabled>Copy ADES</button> <span id="copynote" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>What it does</h2>\n<div class="muted">\n  Photocentres in circular apertures of radius 2.0&ndash;6.0 px in 0.1 px steps, a straight line through x(r) and y(r), and its value at r = 0: the published form of the shrinking-aperture method (Farnocchia et al. 2016, Icarus, arXiv:1507.01980, &sect;2.1 and &sect;3.1; D. J. Tholen). G fits a circular pixel-integrated Gaussian inside each aperture, weighted by exact pixel overlaps, with the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus (the paper\'s default); M is the first moment, re-centred until it stops moving. The paper does not publish its fitting function, so both are offered. Pixels are 0-based. A radius that does not converge has no position; the table gives its reason, and the line is fitted to the rest. The WCS reader handles TAN and TAN-SIP in ICRS (or FK5 at J2000), with HST\'s lookup tables read from the file itself; any other WCS gives pixels only. The ADES record (version 2022) is written only from the header\'s time and your own values, and is a draft for the MPC\'s validator. Where this page says "the command line", it means the same method in Python, in the package this page belongs to (domino-calibrator; <code>pip install .</code> in its folder): <code>domino-calibrator image.fits</code>, the same as <code>python -m calibrator.cli image.fits</code> (see the package\'s <code>README.md</code>); it reads what the page cannot, as each refusal here says. Look at the curve before trusting the intercept.\n</div>\n<div class="muted" id="study">\n  What the study found, &#9733;&#9733; in-house (checked by us, not yet by anyone outside): one comet, 3I/ATLAS, in five visits of Hubble\'s WFC3/UVIS, one filter (F350LP), degraded to 26 ground set-ups, with HST\'s own zero-aperture photocentre as the reference, not the nucleus. With G and the background known, the study\'s setting, the published extrapolation helps in 20 of 20 cells at &le; 1.0&Prime; seeing and &le; 0.44&Prime;/px and fails in 42 of 45 cells at &ge; 2&Prime; seeing and &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; sharp pixels alone are not enough: it fails in 15 of 40 cells at &le; 0.44&Prime;/px with any seeing. With the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus, the page\'s default, G was measured in 50 of the 130 cells, all at &le; 0.70&Prime;/px, and in none at &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; with M, the zero-aperture position is further off than no correction in 58 of the 130 cells, all at &ge; 0.70&Prime;/px, more often than with G (&#9733;, recounted from the same run). The boxes these tallies are counted in were drawn after the data. Check it on your own frames: every cell, the limits and the scripts are in the package\'s <code>README.md</code> and <code>runs/</code>.\n</div>\n\n<footer>A Castle product from Domino Observatory. Built by Annie, the Castle\'s AI.</footer>\n\n<script src="zeroap.js"></script>\n<script>\n(function () {\n  const Z = window.ZeroAp;\n  let hdus = null, cur = null, last = null;    // last: the measurement shown, kept so that the record follows its boxes\n  let hdrTime = null, noteRest = []; }\n  Z.AST_CATS.forEach((c) => { const o = document.createElement(\'option\'); o.value = c; $(\'cats\').appendChild(o); });\n\n  // The answer belongs to the inputs it came from (round 2, 1.10): a new file, HDU, start, estimator, radii or background\n  // clears it and disables Copy; if (!f) return;\n    const my = ++loads;\n    hdus = null; cur = null; hdrTime = null; noteRest = []; clearAnswer();   // a failed load kept the old image, and Measure went on with it (1.10)\n    $(\'hdu\').innerHTML = \'\'; the photocentre-vs-aperture curve, the zero-aperture position and a draft ADES record out. Everything runs in this page; nothing is uploaded.</div>\n\n<h2>1. The cutout</h2>\n<div class="row"><input type="file" id="file" accept=".fits,.fit,.fts,.fz,.gz"> <select id="hdu"></select> <span id="info" class="muted"></span></div>\n<div class="row muted" id="hdrnote"></div>\n<div class="row muted">Click the comet to set the start (default: the brightest 5&times;5 pixel).</div>\n<canvas id="img" width="400" height="400"></canvas>\n<div class="row">start x <input type="number" id="sx" step="0.1"> y <input type="number" id="sy" step="0.1"> <span class="muted">(0-based pixels: x = column, y = row)</span></div>\n\n<h2>2. The method</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>estimator <select id="est"><option value="G">G: symmetric Gaussian fit</option><option value="M">M: moment photocentre</option></select></label>\n  <label>radii from <input type="number" id="r0" value="2.0" step="0.1"> to <input type="number" id="r1" value="6.0" step="0.1"> step <input type="number" id="rs" value="0.1" step="0.05"> px</label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>background <select id="bgm"><option value="annulus">annulus 2.5r&ndash;5r</option><option value="value">known value</option></select></label>\n  <input type="number" id="bgv" value="0" step="any">\n</div>\n\n<h2>3. The record (every value is yours; nothing defaults)</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>station <input type="text" id="stn" placeholder="e.g. 568"></label>\n  <label>designation <input type="text" id="desig" placeholder="3I, 1P, C/2025 N1"></label>\n  <label>mode <select id="mode"><option value="">choose</option><option value="CCD">CCD</option><option value="CMO">CMO (CMOS)</option><option value="VID">VID (video)</option><option value="TDI">TDI</option></select></label>\n  <label>catalogue <input type="text" id="astcat" list="cats" placeholder="e.g. Gaia3"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>submitter <input type="text" id="submitter" style="width:11em"></label>\n  <label>measurers <input type="text" id="measurers" style="width:14em" placeholder="e.g. Smith, J.; Doe, A."></label>\n  <label>observers <input type="text" id="observers" style="width:14em" placeholder="optional; e.g. Roe, B."></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>telescope design <input type="text" id="design" placeholder="e.g. Reflector"></label>\n  <label>aperture (m) <input type="text" id="aperture" style="width:4em"></label>\n  <label>detector <input type="text" id="detector" placeholder="e.g. CMOS"></label>\n  <label>observatory name <input type="text" id="obsname" style="width:11em" placeholder="optional"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row"><label>mid-exposure (UTC) <input type="text" id="tobs" style="width:15em" placeholder="from the header, or type it"></label></div>\n<datalist id="cats"></datalist>\n<div class="row"><button id="run">Measure</button> <span id="status" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>4. The answer</h2>\n<div class="grid">\n  <div><svg id="plot" width="460" height="300" viewBox="0 0 460 300"></svg></div>\n  <div><table id="tab"></table></div>\n</div>\n<div class="row" id="zero"></div>\n<pre id="ades"></pre>\n<div class="row muted" id="adesnote"></div>\n<div class="row"><button id="copy" disabled>Copy ADES</button> <span id="copynote" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>What it does</h2>\n<div class="muted">\n  Photocentres in circular apertures of radius 2.0&ndash;6.0 px in 0.1 px steps, a straight line through x(r) and y(r), and its value at r = 0: the published form of the shrinking-aperture method (Farnocchia et al. 2016, Icarus, arXiv:1507.01980, &sect;2.1 and &sect;3.1; D. J. Tholen). G fits a circular pixel-integrated Gaussian inside each aperture, weighted by exact pixel overlaps, with the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus (the paper\'s default); M is the first moment, re-centred until it stops moving. The paper does not publish its fitting function, so both are offered. Pixels are 0-based. A radius that does not converge has no position; the table gives its reason, and the line is fitted to the rest. The WCS reader handles TAN and TAN-SIP in ICRS (or FK5 at J2000), with HST\'s lookup tables read from the file itself; any other WCS gives pixels only. The ADES record (version 2022) is written only from the header\'s time and your own values, and is a draft for the MPC\'s validator. Where this page says "the command line", it means the same method in Python, in the package this page belongs to (domino-calibrator; <code>pip install .</code> in its folder): <code>domino-calibrator image.fits</code>, the same as <code>python -m calibrator.cli image.fits</code> (see the package\'s <code>README.md</code>); it reads what the page cannot, as each refusal here says. Look at the curve before trusting the intercept.\n</div>\n<div class="muted" id="study">\n  What the study found, &#9733;&#9733; in-house (checked by us, not yet by anyone outside): one comet, 3I/ATLAS, in five visits of Hubble\'s WFC3/UVIS, one filter (F350LP), degraded to 26 ground set-ups, with HST\'s own zero-aperture photocentre as the reference, not the nucleus. With G and the background known, the study\'s setting, the published extrapolation helps in 20 of 20 cells at &le; 1.0&Prime; seeing and &le; 0.44&Prime;/px and fails in 42 of 45 cells at &ge; 2&Prime; seeing and &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; sharp pixels alone are not enough: it fails in 15 of 40 cells at &le; 0.44&Prime;/px with any seeing. With the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus, the page\'s default, G was measured in 50 of the 130 cells, all at &le; 0.70&Prime;/px, and in none at &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; with M, the zero-aperture position is further off than no correction in 58 of the 130 cells, all at &ge; 0.70&Prime;/px, more often than with G (&#9733;, recounted from the same run). The boxes these tallies are counted in were drawn after the data. Check it on your own frames: every cell, the limits and the scripts are in the package\'s <code>README.md</code> and <code>runs/</code>.\n</div>\n\n<footer>A Castle product from Domino Observatory. Built by Annie, the Castle\'s AI.</footer>\n\n<script src="zeroap.js"></script>\n<script>\n(function () {\n  const Z = window.ZeroAp;\n  let hdus = null, cur = null, last = null;    // last: the measurement shown, kept so that the record follows its boxes\n  let hdrTime = null, noteRest = []; the photocentre-vs-aperture curve, the zero-aperture position and a draft ADES record out. Everything runs in this page; nothing is uploaded.</div>\n\n<h2>1. The cutout</h2>\n<div class="row"><input type="file" id="file" accept=".fits,.fit,.fts,.fz,.gz"> <select id="hdu"></select> <span id="info" class="muted"></span></div>\n<div class="row muted" id="hdrnote"></div>\n<div class="row muted">Click the comet to set the start (default: the brightest 5&times;5 pixel).</div>\n<canvas id="img" width="400" height="400"></canvas>\n<div class="row">start x <input type="number" id="sx" step="0.1"> y <input type="number" id="sy" step="0.1"> <span class="muted">(0-based pixels: x = column, y = row)</span></div>\n\n<h2>2. The method</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>estimator <select id="est"><option value="G">G: symmetric Gaussian fit</option><option value="M">M: moment photocentre</option></select></label>\n  <label>radii from <input type="number" id="r0" value="2.0" step="0.1"> to <input type="number" id="r1" value="6.0" step="0.1"> step <input type="number" id="rs" value="0.1" step="0.05"> px</label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>background <select id="bgm"><option value="annulus">annulus 2.5r&ndash;5r</option><option value="value">known value</option></select></label>\n  <input type="number" id="bgv" value="0" step="any">\n</div>\n\n<h2>3. The record (every value is yours; nothing defaults)</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>station <input type="text" id="stn" placeholder="e.g. 568"></label>\n  <label>designation <input type="text" id="desig" placeholder="3I, 1P, C/2025 N1"></label>\n  <label>mode <select id="mode"><option value="">choose</option><option value="CCD">CCD</option><option value="CMO">CMO (CMOS)</option><option value="VID">VID (video)</option><option value="TDI">TDI</option></select></label>\n  <label>catalogue <input type="text" id="astcat" list="cats" placeholder="e.g. Gaia3"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>submitter <input type="text" id="submitter" style="width:11em"></label>\n  <label>measurers <input type="text" id="measurers" style="width:14em" placeholder="e.g. Smith, J.; Doe, A."></label>\n  <label>observers <input type="text" id="observers" style="width:14em" placeholder="optional; e.g. Roe, B."></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>telescope design <input type="text" id="design" placeholder="e.g. Reflector"></label>\n  <label>aperture (m) <input type="text" id="aperture" style="width:4em"></label>\n  <label>detector <input type="text" id="detector" placeholder="e.g. CMOS"></label>\n  <label>observatory name <input type="text" id="obsname" style="width:11em" placeholder="optional"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row"><label>mid-exposure (UTC) <input type="text" id="tobs" style="width:15em" placeholder="from the header, or type it"></label></div>\n<datalist id="cats"></datalist>\n<div class="row"><button id="run">Measure</button> <span id="status" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>4. The answer</h2>\n<div class="grid">\n  <div><svg id="plot" width="460" height="300" viewBox="0 0 460 300"></svg></div>\n  <div><table id="tab"></table></div>\n</div>\n<div class="row" id="zero"></div>\n<pre id="ades"></pre>\n<div class="row muted" id="adesnote"></div>\n<div class="row"><button id="copy" disabled>Copy ADES</button> <span id="copynote" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>What it does</h2>\n<div class="muted">\n  Photocentres in circular apertures of radius 2.0&ndash;6.0 px in 0.1 px steps, a straight line through x(r) and y(r), and its value at r = 0: the published form of the shrinking-aperture method (Farnocchia et al. 2016, Icarus, arXiv:1507.01980, &sect;2.1 and &sect;3.1; D. J. Tholen). G fits a circular pixel-integrated Gaussian inside each aperture, weighted by exact pixel overlaps, with the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus (the paper\'s default); M is the first moment, re-centred until it stops moving. The paper does not publish its fitting function, so both are offered. Pixels are 0-based. A radius that does not converge has no position; the table gives its reason, and the line is fitted to the rest. The WCS reader handles TAN and TAN-SIP in ICRS (or FK5 at J2000), with HST\'s lookup tables read from the file itself; any other WCS gives pixels only. The ADES record (version 2022) is written only from the header\'s time and your own values, and is a draft for the MPC\'s validator. Where this page says "the command line", it means the same method in Python, in the package this page belongs to (domino-calibrator; <code>pip install .</code> in its folder): <code>domino-calibrator image.fits</code>, the same as <code>python -m calibrator.cli image.fits</code> (see the package\'s <code>README.md</code>); it reads what the page cannot, as each refusal here says. Look at the curve before trusting the intercept.\n</div>\n<div class="muted" id="study">\n  What the study found, &#9733;&#9733; in-house (checked by us, not yet by anyone outside): one comet, 3I/ATLAS, in five visits of Hubble\'s WFC3/UVIS, one filter (F350LP), degraded to 26 ground set-ups, with HST\'s own zero-aperture photocentre as the reference, not the nucleus. With G and the background known, the study\'s setting, the published extrapolation helps in 20 of 20 cells at &le; 1.0&Prime; seeing and &le; 0.44&Prime;/px and fails in 42 of 45 cells at &ge; 2&Prime; seeing and &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; sharp pixels alone are not enough: it fails in 15 of 40 cells at &le; 0.44&Prime;/px with any seeing. With the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus, the page\'s default, G was measured in 50 of the 130 cells, all at &le; 0.70&Prime;/px, and in none at &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; with M, the zero-aperture position is further off than no correction in 58 of the 130 cells, all at &ge; 0.70&Prime;/px, more often than with G (&#9733;, recounted from the same run). The boxes these tallies are counted in were drawn after the data. Check it on your own frames: every cell, the limits and the scripts are in the package\'s <code>README.md</code> and <code>runs/</code>.\n</div>\n\n<footer>A Castle product from Domino Observatory. Built by Annie, the Castle\'s AI.</footer>\n\n<script src="zeroap.js"></script>\n<script>\n(function () {\n  const Z = window.ZeroAp;\n  let hdus = null, cur = null, last = null;    // last: the measurement shown, kept so that the record follows its boxes\n  let hdrTime = null, noteRest = []; the photocentre-vs-aperture curve, the zero-aperture position and a draft ADES record out. Everything runs in this page; nothing is uploaded.</div>\n\n<h2>1. The cutout</h2>\n<div class="row"><input type="file" id="file" accept=".fits,.fit,.fts,.fz,.gz"> <select id="hdu"></select> <span id="info" class="muted"></span></div>\n<div class="row muted" id="hdrnote"></div>\n<div class="row muted">Click the comet to set the start (default: the brightest 5&times;5 pixel).</div>\n<canvas id="img" width="400" height="400"></canvas>\n<div class="row">start x <input type="number" id="sx" step="0.1"> y <input type="number" id="sy" step="0.1"> <span class="muted">(0-based pixels: x = column, y = row)</span></div>\n\n<h2>2. The method</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>estimator <select id="est"><option value="G">G: symmetric Gaussian fit</option><option value="M">M: moment photocentre</option></select></label>\n  <label>radii from <input type="number" id="r0" value="2.0" step="0.1"> to <input type="number" id="r1" value="6.0" step="0.1"> step <input type="number" id="rs" value="0.1" step="0.05"> px</label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>background <select id="bgm"><option value="annulus">annulus 2.5r&ndash;5r</option><option value="value">known value</option></select></label>\n  <input type="number" id="bgv" value="0" step="any">\n</div>\n\n<h2>3. The record (every value is yours; nothing defaults)</h2>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>station <input type="text" id="stn" placeholder="e.g. 568"></label>\n  <label>designation <input type="text" id="desig" placeholder="3I, 1P, C/2025 N1"></label>\n  <label>mode <select id="mode"><option value="">choose</option><option value="CCD">CCD</option><option value="CMO">CMO (CMOS)</option><option value="VID">VID (video)</option><option value="TDI">TDI</option></select></label>\n  <label>catalogue <input type="text" id="astcat" list="cats" placeholder="e.g. Gaia3"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>submitter <input type="text" id="submitter" style="width:11em"></label>\n  <label>measurers <input type="text" id="measurers" style="width:14em" placeholder="e.g. Smith, J.; Doe, A."></label>\n  <label>observers <input type="text" id="observers" style="width:14em" placeholder="optional; e.g. Roe, B."></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row">\n  <label>telescope design <input type="text" id="design" placeholder="e.g. Reflector"></label>\n  <label>aperture (m) <input type="text" id="aperture" style="width:4em"></label>\n  <label>detector <input type="text" id="detector" placeholder="e.g. CMOS"></label>\n  <label>observatory name <input type="text" id="obsname" style="width:11em" placeholder="optional"></label>\n</div>\n<div class="row"><label>mid-exposure (UTC) <input type="text" id="tobs" style="width:15em" placeholder="from the header, or type it"></label></div>\n<datalist id="cats"></datalist>\n<div class="row"><button id="run">Measure</button> <span id="status" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>4. The answer</h2>\n<div class="grid">\n  <div><svg id="plot" width="460" height="300" viewBox="0 0 460 300"></svg></div>\n  <div><table id="tab"></table></div>\n</div>\n<div class="row" id="zero"></div>\n<pre id="ades"></pre>\n<div class="row muted" id="adesnote"></div>\n<div class="row"><button id="copy" disabled>Copy ADES</button> <span id="copynote" class="muted"></span></div>\n\n<h2>What it does</h2>\n<div class="muted">\n  Photocentres in circular apertures of radius 2.0&ndash;6.0 px in 0.1 px steps, a straight line through x(r) and y(r), and its value at r = 0: the published form of the shrinking-aperture method (Farnocchia et al. 2016, Icarus, arXiv:1507.01980, &sect;2.1 and &sect;3.1; D. J. Tholen). G fits a circular pixel-integrated Gaussian inside each aperture, weighted by exact pixel overlaps, with the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus (the paper\'s default); M is the first moment, re-centred until it stops moving. The paper does not publish its fitting function, so both are offered. Pixels are 0-based. A radius that does not converge has no position; the table gives its reason, and the line is fitted to the rest. The WCS reader handles TAN and TAN-SIP in ICRS (or FK5 at J2000), with HST\'s lookup tables read from the file itself; any other WCS gives pixels only. The ADES record (version 2022) is written only from the header\'s time and your own values, and is a draft for the MPC\'s validator. Where this page says "the command line", it means the same method in Python, in the package this page belongs to (domino-calibrator; <code>pip install .</code> in its folder): <code>domino-calibrator image.fits</code>, the same as <code>python -m calibrator.cli image.fits</code> (see the package\'s <code>README.md</code>); it reads what the page cannot, as each refusal here says. Look at the curve before trusting the intercept.\n</div>\n<div class="muted" id="study">\n  What the study found, &#9733;&#9733; in-house (checked by us, not yet by anyone outside): one comet, 3I/ATLAS, in five visits of Hubble\'s WFC3/UVIS, one filter (F350LP), degraded to 26 ground set-ups, with HST\'s own zero-aperture photocentre as the reference, not the nucleus. With G and the background known, the study\'s setting, the published extrapolation helps in 20 of 20 cells at &le; 1.0&Prime; seeing and &le; 0.44&Prime;/px and fails in 42 of 45 cells at &ge; 2&Prime; seeing and &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; sharp pixels alone are not enough: it fails in 15 of 40 cells at &le; 0.44&Prime;/px with any seeing. With the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus, the page\'s default, G was measured in 50 of the 130 cells, all at &le; 0.70&Prime;/px, and in none at &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; with M, the zero-aperture position is further off than no correction in 58 of the 130 cells, all at &ge; 0.70&Prime;/px, more often than with G (&#9733;, recounted from the same run). The boxes these tallies are counted in were drawn after the data. Check it on your own frames: every cell, the limits and the scripts are in the package\'s <code>README.md</code> and <code>runs/</code>.\n</div>\n\n<footer>A Castle product from Domino Observatory. Built by Annie, the Castle\'s AI.</footer>\n\n<script src="zeroap.js"></script>\n<script>\n(function () {\n  const Z = window.ZeroAp;\n  let hdus = null, cur = null, last = null;    // last: the measurement shown, kept so that the record follows its boxes\n  let hdrTime = null, noteRest = []; } catch (err) { cur = null; clearAnswer(); $(\'info\').textContent = \'Could not read: \' + err.message; }\n  });\n  function unread(h) {                     // why an image HDU was not decoded\n    if (h.tooBig) return \'too big for the page (\' + (h.nx * h.ny / 1e6).toFixed(0) + \' Mpx; the limit is \' + (Z.MAX_PIXELS / 1e6) + \' Mpx): cut out the comet first\';\n    if (h.truncated) return \'the file ends before the image does\';\n    return h.unreadable || \'not read\';\n  }\n  function merged() { return Z.mergeHeaders(hdus[0].header, cur.header); c.width = Math.round(nx * sc); c.height = Math.round(ny * sc);\n    const st = Z.stretch(cur.data), lo = st.lo, hi = st.hi, soft = Math.max((hi - lo) / 200, 1e-12);   // sampled (#10)\n    const im = ctx.createImageData(nx, ny);\n    for (let i = 0; Everything runs in this page; nothing is uploaded. 1. The cutout FITS cutout image Click the comet to set the start (default: the brightest 5×5 pixel). start x y (0-based pixels: x = column, y = row) 2. The method estimator G: symmetric Gaussian fit M: moment photocentre radii from to step px background annulus 2.5r–5r known value value 3. The record (every value is yours; nothing defaults) The observation station The MPC\'s code for your site. designation As the MPC writes it. mode choose CCD CMO (CMOS) VID (video) TDI catalogue The star catalogue your plate solution used. mid-exposure (UTC) From the header when it has one; otherwise type it. The telescope telescope design aperture (m) detector observatory name Measure 4. The answer Copy ADES What it does Photocentres in circular apertures of radius 2.0–6.0 px in 0.1 px steps, a straight line through x(r) and y(r), and its value at r = 0: the published form of the shrinking-aperture method (Farnocchia et al. 2016, Icarus, arXiv:1507.01980, §2.1 and §3.1; D. J. Tholen). G fits a circular pixel-integrated Gaussian inside each aperture, weighted by exact pixel overlaps, with the background from the 2.5r–5r annulus (the paper\'s default); M is the first moment, re-centred until it stops moving. The paper does not publish its fitting function, so both are offered. Pixels are 0-based. A radius that does not converge has no position; the table gives its reason, and the line is fitted to the rest. The WCS reader handles TAN and TAN-SIP in ICRS (or FK5 at J2000), with HST\'s lookup tables read from the file itself; any other WCS gives pixels only. The ADES record (version 2022) is written only from the header\'s time and your own values, and is a draft for the MPC\'s validator. Where this page says "the command line", it means the same method in Python, in the package this page belongs to (domino-calibrator; pip install . in its folder): domino-calibrator image.fits , the same as python -m calibrator.cli image.fits (see the package\'s README.md ); it reads what the page cannot, as each refusal here says. Look at the curve before trusting the intercept. What the study found, ★★ in-house (checked by us, not yet by anyone outside): one comet, 3I/ATLAS, in five visits of Hubble\'s WFC3/UVIS, one filter (F350LP), degraded to 26 ground set-ups, with HST\'s own zero-aperture photocentre as the reference, not the nucleus. With G and the background known, the study\'s setting, the published extrapolation helps in 20 of 20 cells at ≤ 1.0″ seeing and ≤ 0.44″/px and fails in 42 of 45 cells at ≥ 2″ seeing and ≥ 1.0″/px; sharp pixels alone are not enough: it fails in 15 of 40 cells at ≤ 0.44″/px with any seeing. With the background from the 2.5r–5r annulus, the page\'s default, G was measured in 50 of the 130 cells, all at ≤ 0.70″/px, and in none at ≥ 1.0″/px; with M, the zero-aperture position is further off than no correction in 58 of the 130 cells, all at ≥ 0.70″/px, more often than with G (★, recounted from the same run). The boxes these tallies are counted in were drawn after the data. Check it on your own frames: every cell, the limits and the scripts are in the package\'s README.md and runs/ . G fits a circular pixel-integrated Gaussian inside each aperture, weighted by exact pixel overlaps, with the background from the 2.5r&ndash;5r annulus (the paper\'s default); M is the first moment, re-centred until it stops moving. The paper does not publish its fitting function, so both are offered. Pixels are 0-based. A radius that does not converge has no position; the table gives its reason, and the line is fitted to the rest. The WCS reader handles TAN and TAN-SIP in ICRS (or FK5 at J2000), with HST\'s lookup tables read from the file itself; any other WCS gives pixels only. The ADES record (version 2022) is written only from the header\'s time and your own values, and is a draft for the MPC\'s validator. Where this page says "the command line", it means the same method in Python, in the package this page belongs to (domino-calibrator; <code>pip install .</code> in its folder): <code>domino-calibrator image.fits</code>, the same as <code>python -m calibrator.cli image.fits</code> (see the package\'s <code>README.md</code>); it reads what the page cannot, as each refusal here says. With G and the background known, the study\'s setting, the published extrapolation helps in 20 of 20 cells at &le; 1.0&Prime; seeing and &le; 0.44&Prime;/px and fails in 42 of 45 cells at &ge; 2&Prime; seeing and &ge; 1.0&Prime;/px; sharp pixels alone are not enough: it fails in 15 of 40 cells at &le; 0.44&Prime;/px with any seeing.
